.
If we extend
\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}
to
0
on the whole plane
\{\varepsilon\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}

(keeping the same notation) we have

\displaystyle\partial\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}=\mathcal{L}_{k}+\mathbb{S}({\mathcal{H}}_{k,\varepsilon})+\mathbb{S}(L)_{1}-\mathbb{S}(L)_{2}\qquad\text{ on }\{\varepsilon\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2},

(8.15)

for some current
\mathcal{L}_{k}
supported on
\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}
and whose boundary is two deltas, with suitable signs, on
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
. In particular
\mathcal{L}_{k}

is the integration between
Y_{1}
to
Y_{2}

on the circle
\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}
.

However there are two arcs which connect these two points, namely (in cylindrical coordinates)

\displaystyle\{\varepsilon\}\times\{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}\}\times\left[\frac{-\Theta_{k}(\varepsilon,1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})}{2},\frac{\Theta_{k}(\varepsilon,1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})}{2}\right]

(8.16)

oriented clockwise
and

\displaystyle\left(\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\right)\setminus\left\{\{\varepsilon\}\times\{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}\}\times\left[\frac{-\Theta_{k}(\varepsilon,1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})}{2},\frac{\Theta_{k}(\varepsilon,1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})}{2}\right]\right\}

(8.17)

oriented counterclockwise.
We have to identify
\mathcal{L}_{k}
with the integration over one of
these two arcs.

Proposition 8.8
.

\mathcal{L}_{k}

is the
counterclockwise
integration over the arc connecting
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
given by
(
8.17
).

Before proving this proposition we anticipate a
useful observation.

Remark 8.9
.

We set

\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}:=\mathbb{S}(E_{k})\cap\{t=\varepsilon\}.

Since
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k})
is the boundary of the integration over
\mathbb{S}(E_{k})
,
the current

\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k})\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits((-1,\varepsilon)\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}})+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}
is
boundaryless in
\mathcal{D}_{2}(C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}))

(with
\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}
suitably oriented). It follows, invoking Corollary
8.5
, that

\displaystyle\partial\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}=-\mathbb{S}({\mathcal{H}}_{k,\varepsilon})-\mathbb{S}(L)_{1}+\mathbb{S}(L)_{2}\qquad\text{ in }\{\varepsilon\}\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}.

The fact that

\displaystyle\partial\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}=\mathcal{L}_{k}+\mathbb{S}({\mathcal{H}}_{k,\varepsilon})+\mathbb{S}(L)_{1}-\mathbb{S}(L)_{2}\qquad\text{ in }\{\varepsilon\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2},

(8.18)

(where
\mathcal{L}_{k}
is as in Proposition

8.8
) means that

\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}

is the integration over the set

B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}.

In particular
\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}

has coefficient
1
in
B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}
and zero in
\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}
.
On the other hand, if
\mathcal{L}_{k}
were the integration over
(
8.16
) oriented clockwise,
then we would have that

\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}
had coefficient
-1
in
\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}
and
0
in
B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}
.

We can now prove Proposition
8.8
.

Proof.

Appealing to Remark
8.9
, it is sufficient to show that
the coefficient of
\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}
is

1
in
B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}
. Equivalently we can show that this coefficient is zero in
B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\cap\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}
.

Let us recall, by definitions (
5.26
) and (
5.27
),

\displaystyle(\mathcal{Y}_{k})_{t}=\widetilde{\tau}_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{[1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}]\times((\Omega\setminus D_{k})\cap\partial{\rm B}_{t})}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\qquad{\rm for~a.e.}~t\in(0,\varepsilon],

(8.19)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{X}_{k})_{t}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{t\}\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}})}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}-(\mathcal{Y}_{k})_{t}\qquad\ \ \qquad{\rm for~a.e.}~t\in(0,\varepsilon].

(8.20)

Recalling
Lemma

4.2
(i), we now divide our analysis in two cases:

(1)

|u_{k}(0)|<1-\lambda_{k}
.

(2)

|u_{k}(0)|>1-\lambda_{k}
.

We notice that, in both cases, by continuity of
u_{k}
, for
all
\delta\in(0,1)
there is
t_{k}^{\delta}>0
such that

\displaystyle u_{k}({\rm B}_{t})\subset B_{\delta}(u_{k}(0))\qquad\forall t\in(0,{t_{k}^{\delta}}].

(8.21)

Case (1):

If
\delta
is sufficiently small, we can also assume that

B_{\delta}(u_{k}(0))\subset B_{1-\lambda_{k}}(0),

(8.22)

and therefore

\displaystyle u_{k}({\rm B}_{t})\subset B_{1-\lambda_{k}}(0)\qquad\forall t\in(0,{t_{k}^{\delta}}].

(8.23)

In this case it turns out that if
t\leq t_{k}^{\delta}
then the
current
(\mathcal{Y}_{k})_{t}
in (
8.19
) is null,
because
|u_{k}|^{+}(t)<1-\lambda_{k}
, hence

(\Omega\setminus D_{k})\cap\partial{\rm B}_{t}=\mathchar 31\relax

by (
6.2
). In particular, by (
8.20
),

\displaystyle(\mathcal{X}_{k})_{t}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{t\}\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}})}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\qquad{\rm for~a.e.~}t\leq t_{k}^{\delta}.

Eventually, since
\overline{{\rm B}}_{t}\subset D_{k}

for any
t\in[0,t_{k}^{\delta}]
,
from (
8.21
), we also deduce

u_{k}(\overline{{\rm B}}_{t^{\delta}_{k}}\cap D_{k})=u_{k}(\overline{{\rm B}}_{t_{k}^{\delta}})\subset B_{\delta}(u_{k}(0))
, so that

\displaystyle\Psi_{k}(D_{k})\cap([0,t_{k}^{\delta}]\times B_{1})=\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k}(D_{k})\cap([0,t_{k}^{\delta}]\times B_{1})\subset[0,t_{k}^{\delta}]\times B_{\delta}(u_{k}(0)).

(8.24)

Now, consider
the decomposition (
5.30
) of
\mathcal{E}_{k}
.
By the crucial identification (
5.29
) and (
8.22
) we infer that there must be a set
E_{k,h}\in\{E_{k,i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
with
44
44

44

Since the decomposition in (
5.30
) is done in undecomposable components, such a set is unique.

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{k}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits((-1,t_{k}^{\delta})\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(-1,t_{k}^{\delta})\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}})}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}

\displaystyle=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{E_{k,h}\cap\big((-1,t_{k}^{\delta})\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}})\big)}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}.

Therefore

\displaystyle E_{k,h}\cap\big((-1,t_{k}^{\delta})\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}})\big)=(-1,t_{k}^{\delta})\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}}).

This has the following consequence: denoting as usual

\mathbb{S}(E_{k,h})
the cylindrical symmetrization of
E_{k,h}
we infer

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}(E_{k,h})\cap\big((-1,t_{k}^{\delta})\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}})\big)=(-1,t_{k}^{\delta})\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}}),

and since
\mathbb{S}(E_{k,h})\subset\mathbb{S}(E_{k})
we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}(E_{k})\cap\big((-1,t_{k}^{\delta})\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}})\big)=(-1,t_{k}^{\delta})\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}}).

(8.25)

We now consider two subcases.

(1A)

\mathcal{H}^{2}\Big(\big(\{\varepsilon\}\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\big)\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}\Big)>0
. To conclude the proof it is sufficient to show that

\displaystyle\text{the multiplicity of }\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}\text{ on }\big(\{\varepsilon\}\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\big)\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}\text{ is }1,

(8.26)

because
(\{\varepsilon\}\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}})\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}
is,
by definition, outside the finite perimeter set
\mathbb{S}(E_{k})
.

We argue by slicing, and consider the lines
l_{\rho,\theta}
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
given by
l_{\rho,\theta}=\mathbb{R}\times\{\rho\}\times\{\theta\}
, with
\rho
and
\theta
fixed.
Consider any point
p_{0}
of coordinates
\rho\in(1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})
and
\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]
such that

\displaystyle p_{0}\in(\{\varepsilon\}\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}})\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}.

(8.27)

For a.e. such
\rho\in(1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})
and
\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]

the slice of
\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits((-1,\varepsilon)\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}})
with respect to this line is the sum of some Dirac deltas with
suitable signs, according to the orientation of
\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}
. Indeed
\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}
is the integration over the boundary of the finite perimeter set
\mathbb{S}(E_{k})
, so it turns out that, for a.e.
\rho\in(1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})
and
\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]
the slice of
\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\mathbb{S}(E_{k})}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}
with respect to the line
l_{\rho,\theta}
is exactly

\displaystyle\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\rho,\theta}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\mathbb{S}(E_{k})\cap l_{\rho,\theta}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

(8.28)

that is the integration over some disjoint intervals. If
p_{1},p_{2},\dots p_{m}
are the intervals endpoints (written in order
45
45

45

p_{1}
is the point closer to
\{\varepsilon\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
on
l_{\rho,\theta}
) and if we assume that the last interval between the points
p_{1}
and
p_{0}=(\varepsilon,\rho,\theta)
is outside
\mathbb{S}(E_{k})
, then it results

\partial\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\rho,\theta}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}=-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i>0\\
i\textrm{ even}\end{subarray}}\delta_{p_{i}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i>0\\
i\textrm{ odd}\end{subarray}}\delta_{p_{i}}.

(8.29)

If instead the last interval
[p_{1},p_{0}]
is inside
\mathbb{S}(E_{k})
we have

\partial\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\rho,\theta}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i>0\\
i\textrm{ even}\end{subarray}}\delta_{p_{i}}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i>0\\
i\textrm{ odd}\end{subarray}}\delta_{p_{i}}.

(8.30)

Let us now prove claim (
8.26
).
We have obtained that, for a.e.

\rho\in(1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})
and
any
\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]
such that (
8.27
) holds,
the slice
\partial\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\rho,\theta}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}
is the sum in (
8.29
), and thanks to (
8.25
) we deduce that the total number of points involved in (
8.29
) must be odd. As a
consequence, the push-forward by
\Pi_{\varepsilon}
of
\partial\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\rho,\theta}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}
is a Dirac delta with coefficient
-1
. Since this holds for a.e.
\rho\in(1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})
and any
\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]
, the conclusion follows.

(1B)

Suppose
\mathcal{H}^{2}\Big(\big(\{\varepsilon\}\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\big)\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}\Big)=0
.
In this case we pass to the complementary set; namely, if
\{\varepsilon\}\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}})=\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}\cap\big(\{\varepsilon\}\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\big)
, up to
\mathcal{H}^{2}
-negligible sets, we show that the multiplicity of
\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}
on this set is null. To do so it is sufficient to repeat the slicing argument
above for a.e.
(\rho,\theta)
such that
p_{0}=(\varepsilon,\rho,\theta)\in\{\varepsilon\}\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}})
. For these points (
8.30
) takes place, since by (
8.25
) the number of points involved in the sum is even. The conclusion follows.

Case (2):
Choosing
\delta\in(0,1)
small enough,

\displaystyle\Psi_{k}({\rm B}_{t_{k}^{\delta}})\subset[0,t_{k}^{\delta}]\times B_{\delta}(u_{k}(0)),

(8.31)

and, using
|u_{k}(0)|>1-\lambda_{k}
,

\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k}(D_{k}\cap{\rm B}_{t_{k}^{\delta}})\subset[0,t_{k}^{\delta}]\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}}).

Recalling the definition of
\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}
, it is not difficult to see that the current
\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits((-1,t_{k}^{\delta})\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}})
is supported in
[0,t_{k}^{\delta}]\times(\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}})
.
By the properties of cylindrical symmetrization, we have also that
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k})\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits((-1,t_{k}^{\delta})\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}})
is supported in
[0,t_{k}^{\delta}]\times(\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}})
.

Obviously, being
\mathcal{Y}_{k}
null on
(-1,0)\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}
, we
have

\mathcal{X}_{k}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\Big((-1,0)\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\Big)=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(-1,0)\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}})}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

and we find a set
E_{k,h}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{k}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\Big((-1,0)\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\Big)=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{E_{k,h}\cap((-1,0)\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}}))}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}.

If we pass to the symmetrized set, arguing as in case (1A), we infer

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}(E_{k})\cap\big((-1,0)\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\big)=(-1,0)\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}}).

In other words,

(-1,0)\times(B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}})
is contained
in
\mathbb{S}(E_{k})
, and since the support of
\partial^{*}\mathbb{S}(E_{k})
does not intersect the set
(-1,0)\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}
, we infer that also

\displaystyle(-1,0)\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}\subset\mathbb{S}(E_{k}).

(8.32)

We now decompose
\{\varepsilon\}\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}}
as

\displaystyle\{\varepsilon\}\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}}=\big((\{\varepsilon\}\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\cap\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}\big)\cup\big((\{\varepsilon\}\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}\big),

and one of these two sets on the right-hand side must have positive
\mathcal{H}^{2}
-measure.
Assume that
\mathcal{H}^{2}((\{\varepsilon\}\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon})>0
.
Then we will prove that the multiplicity of
\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}
on this set is
1
(if instead
(\{\varepsilon\}\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}
has zero measure then it is sufficient to prove that
\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}
has zero multiplicity on
(\{\varepsilon\}\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\cap\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}
; we drop this case being completely similar to the former).

Therefore we now proceed as in case (1),
slicing with respect to lines
l_{\rho,\theta}

with
(\varepsilon,\rho,\theta)\in\{\varepsilon\}\times(\{\varepsilon\}\times B_{1-\lambda_{k}})\setminus\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}
. Since the last point

p_{0}=(\varepsilon,\rho,\theta)
does not belong to
\mathbb{S}(E_{k})_{\varepsilon}
, we are concerned with the sum in (
8.29
), and by (
8.32
) we infer that the number of
\{p_{i}\}
involved in the sum is odd. The conclusion follows as in case (1).
∎

9
Gluing rectifiable sets

In this section we
show that, up to adding to
\partial\mathbb{S}(E_{k})
a rectifiable set
with small
\mathcal{H}^{2}
-measure,
\partial\mathbb{S}(E_{k})
can be
described as a polar graph of a suitable modification
of the function
\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}
over a subset
46
46

46

called
S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}
, see (
9.4
).
of the rectangle
47
47

47

In cartesian coordinates.

(0,l)\times[0,1]\times\{0\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
and with Dirichlet boundary conditions independent of
k
. In Section

11
we will
reduce the estimate of the area of the graph of
u_{k}

to an estimate for a non-parametric Plateau problem which in turn will be independent of
k
.

First we remark that
\mathbb{S}(E_{k})\subseteq C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})
and
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k})=\partial\mathbb{S}(\mathcal{E}_{k})
in
C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})
, see
(
5.31
). If we look at
\mathbb{S}(E_{k})
as a subset of
C_{l}
, we cannot conclude
\partial^{*}\mathbb{S}(E_{k})=\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k})
in
C_{l}
, and
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k})
is not a closed current in
C_{l}
. For this reason we have to identify the boundary of
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k})
in
C_{l}
.

Recalling Corollary
8.5
and Definition
7.14
,

\displaystyle{\partial\Big(}\big(\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\big)\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits((\varepsilon,l)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}){\Big)}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits(\{\varepsilon\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial\big(\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k})\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits((\varepsilon,l)\times\mathbb{R}^{2})\big)\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits(\{\varepsilon\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})=-\mathbb{S}({\mathcal{H}}_{k,\varepsilon})-\mathbb{S}(L)_{1}+\mathbb{S}(L)_{2}-\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\overline{Y_{1}Y_{2}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

in
\mathcal{D}_{2}((-\infty,l)\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
, where
\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\overline{Y_{1}Y_{2}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}
is the integration on
{\overline{Y_{1}Y_{2}}}
(see (
8.16
)) oriented from
Y_{1}
to
Y_{2}
.
As a consequence, from (
8.18
), we obtain

\displaystyle\partial\big(\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}\big)\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits(\{\varepsilon\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\qquad\text{ in }\mathcal{D}_{2}((-\infty,l)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}),

where
\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}
is counterclockwisely oriented.

Figure 11:
The largest (resp. smaller) basis circle
has radius
1
(resp.
1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}
). The smallest

1-\lambda_{k}

\mathcal{W}_{k,\varepsilon}

; see (

).

9.1
Enforcing boundary conditions at
\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
;
a modification of
\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}

Let
\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}
denote the integration over the annulus
\{\varepsilon\}\times(B_{1}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}})
, in such a way that

\partial\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}-\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

see Fig.
11
.
Then

\displaystyle|\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}|=\pi(1-(1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})^{2})\leq 2\pi\lambda_{k}^{\prime},

(9.1)

and

\displaystyle\partial\big(\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}+\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}\big)=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\qquad\text{ in }\mathcal{D}_{2}((-\infty,l)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}).

Finally, we add to the current
\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}+\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}
the integration over the lateral boundary of the cylinder
(0,\varepsilon)\times B_{1}
, so that the resulting current

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}+\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\in\mathcal{D}_{2}((0,l)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}),

(9.2)

satisfies

\displaystyle\partial\Big(\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}+\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\Big)=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{0\}\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\text{ in }\mathcal{D}_{2}((-\infty,l)\times\mathbb{R}^{2});

in particular it is boundaryless in

\mathcal{D}_{2}((0,l)\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

Now, we want to identify the solid
region that we can call the “inside” of the current in (
9.2
).

Definition 9.1

(
The sets
O_{k,\varepsilon}
)
.

We let

O_{k,\varepsilon}:=\big(\mathbb{S}(E_{k})\cap((\varepsilon,l)\times\mathbb{R}^{2})\big)\cup((0,\varepsilon]\times B_{1})\subset[0,l]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}.

(9.3)

A direct check shows that the current built in
(
9.2
) is the integration over the boundary of
\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{O_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}
.
Indeed, by Lemma
7.13
(iii) and Definition
7.14

we see that the integration over

\mathbb{S}(E_{k})\cap((\varepsilon,l)\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
has as boundary
\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}
in
(\varepsilon,l)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
whereas
(0,\varepsilon]\times B_{1}
trivially
has boundary
(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}
in
(0,\varepsilon)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
The current
\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}+\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}
represents the boundary of
\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{O_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}
concentrated on the plane
\{\varepsilon\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
In turn we will see (formulas (
9.6
),
(
9.7
)) that
O_{k,\varepsilon}
is the polar subgraph of
a suitable modification of
\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}
.
Thus we are going to introduce the new extra “strip” (recalling
the definition of
S^{(2)}_{k,\varepsilon}
in (
7.7
) and of
J_{Q_{k,\varepsilon}}
in
(
7.21
)):

\displaystyle S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}:

\displaystyle=S^{(2)}_{k,\varepsilon}\cup J_{Q_{k,\varepsilon}}\cup((\varepsilon,l)\times[1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime},1]\times\{0\})

(9.4)

\displaystyle=\{(t,\rho,\theta):t\in(\varepsilon,l),\rho\in[|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k}),1],\theta=0\},

see Fig.
12
(and also Figs.
4
,
8
).

Figure 12:
The graphs of the functions
|u_{k}|^{+}
and
|u_{k}|^{-}

and the set
S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}
in (
9.4
).
See also Fig.
4
.

Definition 9.2

(
The function
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}
)
.

We define

\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}:(0,l)\times[0,1]\times\{0\}\to\mathbb{R}
as

\displaystyle\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}:=\begin{cases}\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}&\text{ in }(\varepsilon,l)\times[0,1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}]\times\{0\}\\
0&\text{ in }(\varepsilon,l)\times[1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime},1]\times\{0\}\\
\pi&\text{ in }(0,\varepsilon]\times[0,1]\times\{0\}.\end{cases}

(9.5)

Accordingly, we extend the currents
\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}
and

\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}
as follows:
As in (
7.2
) we fix
\eta\in(0,\frac{\pi}{4})
,
and set

\displaystyle SG^{\rm pol}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}:=\{(t,\rho,\theta)\in(0,l)\times[0,1]\times\{0\}:\theta\in(-\eta,\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}(t,\rho,0))\},

\displaystyle UG^{\rm pol}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}:=\{(t,\rho,\theta)\in(0,l)\times[0,1]\times\{0\}:\theta\in(-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}(t,\rho,0),\eta)\}.

Remark 9.3
.

By construction,

\displaystyle SG^{\rm pol}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}\cap\{\theta\in(0,\pi)\}=O_{k,\varepsilon}\cap\{\theta\in(0,\pi)\},

(9.6)

\displaystyle UG^{\rm pol}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}\cap\{\theta\in(-\pi,0)\}=O_{k,\varepsilon}\cap\{\theta\in(-\pi,0)\},

(9.7)

where the set
O_{k,\varepsilon}
is defined in (
9.3
).

The next currents are constructed to reach the
segment
(0,l)\times\{1\}\times\{0\}
.

Definition 9.4

(
The currents
\mathcal{G}_{\pm\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}
)
.

We define the currents

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}:=(\partial\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{SG^{\rm pol}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}})\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{\theta\in(0,\pi)\}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{G^{\rm pol}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\big(\big\{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}\in\{0,\pi\}\big\}\cap S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}\big)}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

(9.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}:=(\partial\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{UG^{\rm pol}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}})\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{\theta\in(-\pi,0)\}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{G^{\rm pol}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\big(\big\{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}\in\{0,\pi\}\big\}\cap S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}\big)}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}.

In other words, the support of
\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}
coincides with the
generalized polar graph of
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}
restricted to
S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}\times[0,\pi]
.
Notice that also in this case
\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{G^{\rm pol}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\big(\big\{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}\in\{0,\pi\}\big\}\cap S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}\big)}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{G^{\rm pol}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\big(\big\{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}\in\{0,\pi\}\big\}\cap S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}\big)}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}=0
,
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\partial^{*}O_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\text{ in }(0,l)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}.

(9.9)

Moreover, by (
9.8
)
and (
7.9
),

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|+|\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|=|\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|+2\mathcal{H}^{2}\left(\big\{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}\in\{0,\pi\}\big\}\cap S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}\right).

(9.10)

Finally

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(\varepsilon,l)\times[1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime},1]\times\{0\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}\cap\{0\leq\theta\leq\pi\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{0\leq\theta\leq\pi\}

\displaystyle+{\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{0\leq\theta\leq\pi\}}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{((0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1})\cap\{0\leq\theta\leq\pi\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

(9.11)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}-\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(\varepsilon,l)\times[1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime},1]\times\{0\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}\cap\{-\pi\leq\theta\leq 0\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{-\pi\leq\theta\leq 0\}

\displaystyle+{\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{-\pi\leq\theta\leq 0\}}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{((0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1})\cap\{-\pi\leq\theta\leq 0\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

so that

\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}=\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}+\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}.

Remark 9.5
.

The function
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}
is defined on the whole domain
(0,l)\times[0,1]\times\{0\}
, but it might take values in
(0,\pi)
only in

S^{(2)}_{k,\varepsilon}
, see Remark
7.5
(v). Moreover, referring also to Remark
7.8
, we see that the currents
\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}
and
\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}
neglect the generalized polar graph of
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}

(defined in (
2.8
))
on
((0,l)\times[0,1]\times\{0\})\setminus S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}
, with the only
exception of the “vertical” part
\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}
.

An important step in the proof of the estimate
from below in Theorem
11.16
is given by the
next inequality.

Proposition 9.6

(
Estimate from below in terms of the mass of
\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}
and
\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}
)
.

Let
\varepsilon
be fixed as in (
4.4
) and (
4.5
).
The following inequality holds:

\displaystyle|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{G_{u_{k}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|_{D_{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\geq|\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|+|\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|-\pi\varepsilon-\frac{C}{\varepsilon n}-o_{k}(1)

(9.12)

for an absolute constant
C>0
, where the sequence

o_{k}(1)\geq 0
depends on
\varepsilon
and
n
, and is infinitesimal as
k\rightarrow+\infty
.

Proof.

By (
9.1
) we get

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|\leq

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}|+\lambda_{k}^{\prime}l+|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}\cap\{0<\theta<\pi\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|+|\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{0<\theta<\pi\}|

\displaystyle+|{\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{0\leq\theta\leq\pi\}}|+|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{((0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1})\cap\{0\leq\theta\leq\pi\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|.

A similar estimate holds for
|\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|
so that

|\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|+|\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|\leq|\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}|+|\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}|+|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|+|\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}|+|\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}|+|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|+2\lambda_{k}^{\prime}l.

Coupling the above inequality with
(
7.22
) and (
7.23
) gives

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|+|\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{G}}^{+}_{k,\varepsilon}|+|{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}|+|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|+|\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}|+|\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}|+|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|+2\lambda_{k}^{\prime}l+\frac{1}{\pi\varepsilon n}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{D_{k}\cap(\Omega\setminus\overline{{\rm B}}_{\varepsilon})}|J(\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})|dx+|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|+|\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}|+|\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}|+|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|

\displaystyle\quad+2\lambda_{k}^{\prime}l+\frac{1}{\pi\varepsilon n}+\frac{1}{n}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{D_{k}\cap(\Omega\setminus\overline{{\rm B}}_{\varepsilon})}|J(\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})|dx+|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{G_{u_{k}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|_{(D_{k}\cap{\rm B}_{\varepsilon})\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}+\pi\varepsilon+\frac{C}{\varepsilon n}+o_{k}(1),

where the second inequality follows from
(
7.12
), the last inequality follows from
(
7.27
), (
9.1
)
and (
8.13
), and
C>0
is an absolute constant. Here
o_{k}(1)
is a nonnegative quantity
infinitesimal as
k\rightarrow+\infty
, depending on
\varepsilon
and
n
.
In conclusion

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|+|\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|-\pi\varepsilon-\frac{C}{\varepsilon n}-o_{k}(1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{G_{u_{k}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|_{(D_{k}\cap(\Omega\setminus{\rm B}_{\varepsilon}))\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}+|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{G_{u_{k}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|_{(D_{k}\cap{\rm B}_{\varepsilon})\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}=|\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{G_{u_{k}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}|_{D_{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}.

∎

10
Three examples

Before concluding the proof of the lower bound,
we aim to explain the various geometric objects
introduced in the previous sections (for a recovery sequence) through three
interesting examples of sequences converging to the vortex map
u
.
We warn the reader that the sequences of Sections

10.1

and
10.2
, as well as the one of Section

10.3
for
l

small, are not recovery sequences; nevertheless, we believe
it is useful to describe the various quantities introduced in
Sections
5
-
9
in correspondence
to these sequences, since this shades some light
on the proof of the lower bound.

10.1
An approximating sequence of maps with degree zero: cylinder

In
[
1
]
the authors describe
an approximating sequence
(u_{k})
of smooth maps taking values
in
\mathbb{S}^{1}

which, in our context,
are defined in polar coordinates as follows:

{u_{k}}(r,\theta):=\begin{cases}(\cos\theta,\sin\theta)&\textrm{ \qquad in }\Omega_{1}:={\Omega}\setminus({{\rm B}_{r_{k}}}\cup\{\theta\in(-\theta_{k},\theta_{k})\}),\\
(\cos(\frac{r}{{r_{k}}}(\theta-\pi)+\pi),\sin(\frac{r}{{r_{k}}}(\theta-\pi)+\pi))&\textrm{ \qquad in }{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}}\setminus\{\theta\in(-\theta_{k},\theta_{k})\},\\
(\cos(\frac{\theta_{k}-\pi}{\theta_{k}}\theta+\pi),\sin(\frac{\theta_{k}-\pi}{\theta_{k}}\theta+\pi))&\textrm{ \qquad in }\{\theta\in[0,\theta_{k})\}\setminus{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}},\\
(\cos(\frac{-\theta_{k}+\pi}{-\theta_{k}}\theta+\pi),\sin(\frac{-\theta_{k}+\pi}{-\theta_{k}}\theta+\pi))&\textrm{ \qquad in }\{\theta\in(-\theta_{k},0)\}\setminus{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}},\\
(\cos(\frac{r}{{r_{k}}}(\frac{\theta_{k}-\pi}{\theta_{k}}\theta)+\pi),\sin(\frac{r}{{r_{k}}}(\frac{\theta_{k}-\pi}{\theta_{k}}\theta)+\pi))&\textrm{ \qquad in }\Omega_{4}:={{\rm B}_{r_{k}}}\cap\{\theta\in[0,\theta_{k})\},\\
(\cos(\frac{r}{{r_{k}}}(\frac{-\theta_{k}+\pi}{-\theta_{k}}\theta)+\pi),\sin(\frac{r}{{r_{k}}}(\frac{-\theta_{k}+\pi}{-\theta_{k}}\theta)+\pi))&\textrm{ \qquad in }\Omega_{4}:={{\rm B}_{r_{k}}}\cap\{\theta\in(-\theta_{k},\theta_{k})\},\end{cases}

(10.1)

where
({r_{k}})
and
(\theta_{k})
are two infinitesimal sequences
of positive numbers; see Fig.
13
.
Recall that in the previous sections
we introduced the number
\varepsilon
;
we may assume here that

{r_{k}}<<\varepsilon

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Notice that

u_{k}(0,0)=(-1,0)=u_{k}(r,0)
for
r\in(0,l)
.
Moreover for
t\in(0,l)
we have
u_{k}(\partial{\rm B}_{t})=\partial B_{1}\setminus\{\theta\in(-\theta_{k},\theta_{k})\}
, and the degree
of
u_{k}
is zero.

Now we fix an infinitesimal sequence
(\lambda_{k})
of positive
numbers. Inspecting (
10.1
),
it turns out that the set
D_{k}
defined in (
4.27
)
and (
4.9
) satisfies

D_{k}\subsetneq{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}}\cup\{\theta\in(-\theta_{k},\theta_{k})\}\cap\Omega
,
see Fig.
14
.

Figure 13:

The map
u_{k}
in (
10.1
).
We set
\hat{P}:=P/|P|=\theta_{k}
,

\hat{Q}:=Q/|Q|
. All points in

on

.

through

\mathbb{S}^{1}
.

\Omega_{4}
through

is as follows:

u_{k}
sends the generic dotted segment onto the
(short) dotted arc on
\mathbb{S}^{1}
.

Figure 14:
The set emphasized is
D_{k}

for the sequence in (
10.1
).

Notice that
{\rm spt}({u_{k}}_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(\Omega\setminus D_{k})\cap\partial{\rm B}_{t}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}})\subseteq u_{k}((\Omega\setminus D_{k})\cap\partial{\rm B}_{t})
with strict inclusion
whenever
t
is such that
(\Omega\setminus\overline{D_{k}})\cap\{\theta=\pm\theta_{k}\}\cap\partial{\rm B}_{t}\neq\mathchar 31\relax
.
For instance, for
t\in({r_{k}},l),
we have
u_{k}((\Omega\setminus D_{k})\cap\partial{\rm B}_{t})=\partial B_{1}\setminus\{\theta\in(-\theta_{k},\theta_{k})\}
and

u_{k}(D_{k}\cap\partial{\rm B}_{t})=\partial B_{1}\setminus\{\theta\in(-\theta_{k}-a_{k},\theta_{k}+a_{k})\}
,
where
a_{k}
is a positive small angle;
on the other hand
48
48

48

This is due to the fact that

u_{k}|_{\Omega\setminus D_{k}}
covers the arcs

(\partial B_{1})\cap\{\theta\in(\theta_{k},\theta_{k}+a_{k})\cup(-\theta-a_{k},-\theta)\}
twice, with opposite orientations.

{\rm spt}({u_{k}}_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(\Omega\setminus D_{k})\cap\partial{\rm B}_{t}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}})=\partial B_{1}\setminus\{\theta\in(-\theta_{k}-a_{k},\theta_{k}+a_{k})\}={\rm spt}({u_{k}}_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{D_{k}\cap\partial{\rm B}_{t}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}})
. Similarly we have

{\rm spt}({u_{k}}_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{D_{k}\cap\partial{\rm B}_{t}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}})\subseteq u_{k}(D_{k}\cap\partial{\rm B}_{t})

with strict inclusion
49
49

49

This could only happen for
t\in(0,{r_{k}})
.
whenever

D_{k}\cap\{\theta=\pm\theta_{k}\}\cap\partial{\rm B}_{t}\neq\mathchar 31\relax
(thus,
t\in(0,r_{k})
).

The support of the current
{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}=(\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{D_{k}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}

in (
5.6
)
is a connected set contained in
\partial_{{\rm lat}}(C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k}))
.
But in this
example we also have
{\rm spt}({\mathfrak{D}}_{k})={\rm spt}\left((\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Omega\setminus D_{k}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\right)
;
moreover

{\rm spt}({\mathfrak{D}}_{k})\cap C^{\varepsilon}_{l}=(\varepsilon,l)\times\{1-\lambda_{k}\}\times\left((-\pi,\pi)\setminus(-\theta_{k}-a_{k},\theta_{k}+a_{k})\right).

The support of the current

{\mathcal{W}}_{k}
in (
5.17
), an orthogonal “wall”
over
C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k})

of
height
\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}
,
built on
\partial{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}
,
divides
C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})\setminus C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k})
into two connected sets; one of them,

{\rm spt}(\mathcal{Y}_{k})

(see (
5.26
)),
has

as part of its boundary, and the other one,

{\rm spt}(\mathcal{X}_{k})
(see (
5.27
)), has
\partial C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k})\setminus{\rm spt}((\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Omega\setminus D_{k}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}})

as part of its boundary.
Hence the support of
\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}={\mathfrak{D}}_{k}+{\mathcal{W}}_{k}
(Definition
5.13
) divides

\pi&\qquad\text{for }(t,\rho)\in(\varepsilon,l)\times(0,1-\lambda_{k}].\end{cases}

Note that

Remark 10.1
.

(u_{k})
is not a recovery sequence,
due to Theorem
13.2
.
We have

\lim_{k\to+\infty}\mathcal{A}(u_{k},\Omega)=\int_{\Omega}|\mathcal{M}(\nabla u)|~dx+2\pi l,

and
2\pi l
has the meaning of the lateral area of the cylinder of
height
l
and basis the unit disc. This surface is not a minimizer
of the problem on the right-hand side of (
11.25
)
(where it corresponds to
h\equiv 1
).

.
We replace the cylinder lateral surface

We define

in

\displaystyle u_{k}\left(\partial{\rm B}_{t}\cap\{\pm\theta\in(\theta_{k},\overline{\theta_{k}})\}\right)

\displaystyle u_{k}\left(\partial{\rm B}_{t}\cap\{\pm\theta\in(0,\theta_{k})\}\right)

\displaystyle=\{(r,0)\in B_{1}:r\in[\rho(t),1]\}\cup\Big(\partial B_{\rho(t)}\cap\{\pm\theta\in(0,\pi)\}\Big).

.

Remark 10.2
.

For an explicit construction, see Section
13
with
{h^{\star}}=\overline{\rho}
and

Now we fix an infinitesimal sequence
(\lambda_{k})
of positive numbers;
we may also assume that
|(\cos\overline{\theta}_{k},\sin\overline{\theta}_{k})-(1,0)|<<\lambda_{k}
.
Hence
D_{k}\subsetneq({{\rm B}_{r_{k}}}\cup\{\theta\in(-\theta_{k},\theta_{k})\})
.

By construction there exists
t_{k}\in({r_{k}},\varepsilon)

such that
\rho(t_{k})=1-\lambda_{k}
.
Hence for
t\in[t_{k},l)
we have

\displaystyle{\rm spt}\Big((u_{k})_{\sharp}(\partial{\rm B}_{t}\cap(\Omega\setminus D_{k}))\Big)

\displaystyle=\partial B_{1},\qquad\partial B_{1}\subsetneq u_{k}(\partial{\rm B}_{t}\cap(\Omega\setminus D_{k})),

\displaystyle{\rm spt}\Big((u_{k})_{\sharp}(\partial{\rm B}_{t}\cap D_{k})\Big)

\displaystyle=\partial B_{\rho(t)},\qquad\partial B_{\rho(t)}\subsetneq u_{k}(\partial{\rm B}_{t}\cap D_{k}).

Notice that the above strict inclusions are due to the fact that the segments
\{(r,0)\in B_{1}:r\in[\rho(t),1]\}\cap u_{k}(D_{k})
and
\{(r,0)\in B_{1}:r\in[\rho(t),1]\}\cap u_{k}(\Omega\setminus D_{k})

are covered twice with opposite orientation.

On the other hand, for
t\in[{r_{k}},t_{k})
we have

\displaystyle\partial B_{1}

\displaystyle\subset{\rm spt}\Big((u_{k})_{\sharp}(\partial{\rm B}_{t}\cap(\Omega\setminus D_{k}))\Big),\qquad{\rm spt}\Big((\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})_{\sharp}(\partial{\rm B}_{t}\cap(\Omega\setminus D_{k}))\Big)\subset\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}}

\displaystyle\partial B_{\rho(t)}

\displaystyle\supset{\rm spt}\Big((u_{k})_{\sharp}(\partial{\rm B}_{t}\cap D_{k})\Big),\qquad{\rm spt}\Big((\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})_{\sharp}(\partial{\rm B}_{t}\cap D_{k})\Big)\subset\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}},

where the second inclusion is due to the fact that
(\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})(\partial{\rm B}_{t}\cap(\Omega\setminus D_{k}))
covers two arcs of
\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}}
twice with opposite orientation.
Notice also that the only cancellation that could happen on

due to covering more than one with opposite orientation is along the
segment
\{(r,0)\in B_{1}:r\in[\rho(t),1]\}
,
in particular we have

; moreover we have

where

\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k}(0)=\{0\}\times\{1-\lambda_{k}\}\times\{\pi\}
.

From the above discussion we can see that the support of the current
{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}
is a connected set contained in

and the support of

, in particular we have

\displaystyle{\rm spt}\Big({\mathfrak{D}}_{k}\Big)\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits C_{t_{k}}={\rm spt}\Big((\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Omega\setminus D_{k}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\Big)\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits C_{t_{k}}~\subsetneq\partial C_{t_{k}}(1-\lambda_{k}),

\displaystyle{\rm spt}\Big({\mathfrak{D}}_{k}\Big)\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits C^{t_{k}}_{l}=\{(t,r,\theta):t\in(t_{k},l),~r=\rho(t),~\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]\}\subsetneq C_{l}^{t_{k}}(1-\lambda_{k}),

\displaystyle{\rm spt}\Big(\partial{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}\Big)\subset\partial C_{t_{k}}(1-\lambda_{k}),

\displaystyle{\rm spt}\Big((\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Omega\setminus D_{k}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\Big)\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits C^{t_{k}}_{l}=\partial C_{l}^{t_{k}}(1-\lambda_{k}).

The current

, a normal wall over

over

as part of its boundary.

divides

into two connected sets, one of them is

and the second one is
its complement in
C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})
and contains

{\rm spt}(\mathcal{Y}_{k})
.
Note that
{\rm spt}({\mathcal{W}}_{k})\subset[0,t_{k}]\times[1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}]\times(-\pi,\pi]
and that
{\rm spt}(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k})\cap(\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
is the segment

\{0\}\times[1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}]\times\{\pi\}

In this particular example when we apply the cylindrical Steiner symmetrization introduced in Section
3
nothing changes,
i.e.,

\mathbb{S}(E_{k})=E_{k}
and
hence
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k})=\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}
.
Observe that

\displaystyle|u_{k}|^{+}=1,\qquad|u_{k}|^{-}=\begin{cases}1,&r\leq{r_{k}},\\
\rho(t),&r>{r_{k}}.\end{cases}

Thus

\pi_{0}^{\rm pol}(\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k}(\Omega\setminus\overline{{\rm B}}_{\varepsilon}))=\{(t,r,0):t\in(\varepsilon,l)~r\in[\rho(t),1-\lambda_{k}]\},

and the sets

Hence

Moreover we have
\Theta_{k}(t,\rho)=0
for
(t,\rho,0)\in S^{(2)}_{k,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pm\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}={\mathcal{G}}^{\pm}_{k,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{(t,\rho,\theta):t\in(\varepsilon,l),~\pm\theta\in(0,\pi)\}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{G^{\rm pol}_{\pm\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits S^{(2)}_{k,\varepsilon}\cap\{\Theta_{k}\in\{0,2\pi\}\}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{(t,\rho,\theta):t\in(\varepsilon,l),~\pm\theta\in(0,\pi)\}\pm\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{(t,r,0):t\in(\varepsilon,l),~r\in[\rho(t),1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}]\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}.

The set
\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}
in Definition
7.14
is empty,
hence
\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}=\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}
which is boundaryless in
C^{\varepsilon}_{l}
.

We have, recalling (
8.12
),

\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times[\rho(\varepsilon),1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}]\times(-\pi,\pi]}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

i.e.,

\partial\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}=\mathbb{S}({\mathcal{H}}_{k,\varepsilon})+\mathbb{S}(L)_{1}-\mathbb{S}(L)_{2}+\mathcal{L}_{k}=-\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{\rho(\varepsilon)}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

where
(see (
8.10
) (
8.4
), (
8.8
), (
8.15
), and Proposition
8.8
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}({\mathcal{H}}_{k,\varepsilon})

\displaystyle={\mathcal{H}}_{k,\varepsilon}=(\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{D_{k}\cap\partial{\rm B}_{\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\text{ (oriented counterclockwise)}=-\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{\rho(\varepsilon)}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}(L)_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{S}(L)_{2}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times[1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}]\times\{0\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{k}

\displaystyle=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\overline{Y_{2}Y_{1}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}.

Notice that

\displaystyle\partial(\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)})\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{\varepsilon\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}

\displaystyle=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}

\displaystyle=-\Big(\mathbb{S}({\mathcal{H}}_{k,\varepsilon})+\mathbb{S}(L)_{1}-\mathbb{S}(L)_{2}\Big)+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}-\mathcal{L}_{k}.

Thus

\partial(\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon})\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits(\{\varepsilon\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pm\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pm\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\Big(\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}+\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\Big)\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{0\leq\pm\theta\leq\pi\}\pm\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(\varepsilon,l)\times[1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime},1]\times\{0\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

where
\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times(B_{1}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}})}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}
;
observe that

(\partial B_{1}\cap\{0\leq\pm\theta\leq\pi\})\cup\{[0,l]\times\{1\}\times\{0\}\}\subset{\rm supp}(\partial\mathcal{G}_{\pm\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}).

Hence

\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}=\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}+\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

and

\partial(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{t<l\}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{0\}\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}.

Remark 10.3
.

(u_{k})
is not a recovery sequence,
due to Theorem
13.2
.
We have

\lim_{k\to+\infty}\mathcal{A}(u_{k},\Omega)=\int_{\Omega}|\mathcal{M}(\nabla u)|~dx+\mathcal{H}^{2}({\rm catenoid})+2\,\mathcal{H}^{2}({\rm flap}).

This surface is not a minimizer
of problem on the right-hand side of (
11.25
).

10.3
Smoothing by convolution: the case of the two discs

In
[
1
]
, the authors describe
a sequence
(u_{k})
of maps converging to
the vortex map
u
, simply defined as follows:

{u_{k}}(r,\theta):=\phi_{k}(r)u(r,\theta),

(10.2)

where
\phi_{k}:[0,l]\to[0,1]
is a smooth function
such that
\phi_{k}=0
in
[0,\frac{1}{k^{2}}]
,

\phi_{k}=1
in
[\frac{1}{k},l]
,
and
0\leq\phi_{k}^{\prime}\leq 2k
. Hence
|u_{k}-u|=1-\phi_{k}
.
We shall assume that for all
k>0
, we have
\frac{1}{k}<<\varepsilon
.

Now we fix an infinitesimal sequence
(\lambda_{k})
of positive
numbers, hence for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
r_{k}\in(0,1/k)

such that
\phi_{k}(r_{k})=1-\lambda_{k}
, and we have

D_{k}={\rm B}_{r_{k}}.

(10.3)

Notice that
u_{k}(\partial{\rm B}_{r})=\partial B_{\phi_{k}(r)}
,

\displaystyle{\rm spt}({\mathfrak{D}}_{k})=\{(t,r,\theta):t\in[1/k^{2},r_{k}],~r=\phi_{k}(t),\;\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]\}\subsetneq\overline{C}_{r_{k}}(1-\lambda_{k}),

\displaystyle{\rm spt}((\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Omega\setminus D_{k}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}})=\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k}(\Omega\setminus D_{k})=\partial C^{r_{k}}_{l}(1-\lambda_{k}).

Also, using (
10.3
),

\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k}(D_{k})\setminus{\rm spt}({\mathfrak{D}}_{k})=[0,1/k^{2})\times\{0\}\times\{0\}=\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k}({\rm B}_{1/k^{2}})
and,
unlike the examples in Sections

10.1
,
10.2
, there is no cancellation due to
covering the same
2
-dimensional
set with two opposite orientations; the fact
that
\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k}(D_{k})\setminus{\rm spt}({\mathfrak{D}}_{k})

is nonempty is due to the fact that

\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k}({\rm B}_{1/k^{2}})
is one-dimensional.
Moreover we have

{\rm spt}((\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\partial D_{k}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}})={\rm spt}({\mathfrak{D}}_{k})\cap{\rm spt}((\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k})_{\sharp}\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Omega\setminus D_{k}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}})=\{r_{k}\}\times\{1-\lambda_{k}\}\times(-\pi,\pi].

Hence

{\rm spt}({\mathcal{W}}_{k})=\{r_{k}\}\times[1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}]\times(-\pi,\pi],

i.e.,
the current

{\mathcal{W}}_{k}

divides
C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})\setminus C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k})
into two connected sets; one of them,

{\rm spt}(\mathcal{Y}_{k})=C^{r_{k}}_{l}(1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})\setminus C^{r_{k}}_{l}(1-\lambda_{k})
, and the other one,

{\rm spt}(\mathcal{X}_{k})=C_{r_{k}}(1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})\setminus C_{r_{k}}(1-\lambda_{k})
.
Therefore
\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}={\mathfrak{D}}_{k}+{\mathcal{W}}_{k}
divides
C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})
into two connected sets, one of them is

E_{k}=C_{r_{k}}(1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})\setminus\{(t,r,\theta):t\in(1/k^{2},r_{k}),~r\in(0,\phi_{k}(t)),~\theta\in(-\pi,\pi]\},

and the second one is
its complement in
C_{l}(1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime})
and contains

{\rm spt}(\mathcal{Y}_{k})
.

Also in this example,
when we apply the cylindrical Steiner symmetrization
introduced in Section
3
, nothing changes,
i.e.,

\mathbb{S}(E_{k})=E_{k}
and
hence
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k})=\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{k}
.

Note also that
|u_{k}|^{+}=|u_{k}|^{-}=1
in
(r_{k},l)
, thus
\pi_{0}^{\rm pol}(\pi_{\lambda_{k}}\circ\Psi_{k}(\Omega\setminus\overline{{\rm B}}_{\varepsilon}))=(\varepsilon,l)\times\{1-\lambda_{k}\}\times\{0\}
, and the sets
Q_{k,\varepsilon}

in (
6.4
) and
J_{Q_{k,\varepsilon}}
in (
7.21
) are empty.
Hence
S^{(2)}_{k,\varepsilon}=(\varepsilon,l)\times[1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}]\times\{0\}
.
We have
\Theta_{k}(t,\rho)=0
for
(t,\rho,0)\in S^{(2)}_{k,\varepsilon}
thus,
recalling Definitions
7.7

and
7.12
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pm\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}={\mathcal{G}}^{\pm}_{k,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\pm\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{S^{(2)}_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}=\pm\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(\varepsilon,l)\times[1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}]\times\{0\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}.

The set
\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}
in Definition
7.14
is empty,
hence
\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\Sigma_{k,\varepsilon}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}=0
.
We have, recalling (
8.12
),

\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\overline{B}_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

and

\partial\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}=\mathbb{S}({\mathcal{H}}_{k,\varepsilon})+\mathbb{S}(L)_{1}-\mathbb{S}(L)_{2}+\mathcal{L}_{k}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

where (see (
8.10
) (
8.4
), (
8.8
), (
8.15
), and Proposition
8.8
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}({\mathcal{H}}_{k,\varepsilon})={\mathcal{H}}_{k,\varepsilon}=0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}(L)_{1}=\mathbb{S}(L)_{2}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times[1-\lambda_{k},1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}]\times\{0\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{k}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}.

Notice that

\partial(\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)})\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits(\{\varepsilon\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})=-\Big(\mathbb{S}({\mathcal{H}}_{k,\varepsilon})+\mathbb{S}(L)_{1}-\mathbb{S}(L)_{2}\Big)+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}-\mathcal{L}_{k}=0,

and

\partial(\mathcal{G}_{\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon})\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits(\{\varepsilon\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\partial B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}.

Finally we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pm\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pm\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(3)}+\Big(\mathcal{V}_{k,\varepsilon}+\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\Big)\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{0\leq\pm\theta\leq\pi\}\pm\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(\varepsilon,l)\times[1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime},1]\times\{0\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\overline{B}_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}\pm\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(\varepsilon,l)\times[1-\lambda_{k},1]\times\{0\}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

where
\mathsf{a}_{k,\varepsilon}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times(B_{1}\setminus B_{1-\lambda_{k}^{\prime}})}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}
.
Notice that

(\partial B_{1}\cap\{0\leq\pm\theta\leq\pi\})\cup\{[0,l]\times\{1\}\times\{0\}\}\subset{\rm supp}(\{\partial\mathcal{G}_{\pm\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}\}).

Hence

\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{\varepsilon\}\times\overline{B}_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}+\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{(0,\varepsilon)\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

and

\partial(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits\{t<l\}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{\{0\}\times\partial B_{1}}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}}.

Remark 10.4
.

(u_{k})
is a recovery sequence for
l

sufficiently large, due to
[
1
, Lemma 4.2]
.
We have

\lim_{k\to+\infty}\mathcal{A}(u_{k},\Omega)=\int_{\Omega}|\mathcal{M}(\nabla u)|~dx+\pi,

and
\pi
has the meaning of the area of the unit disc.
This surface, for
l
sufficiently large, is a minimizer
of problem on the right-hand side of (
11.25
)
(where it corresponds to
h\equiv-1
).

11
Lower bound

In this section we reduce the analysis of

\mathcal{G}_{\pm\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}
in Definition
9.4

to a non-parametric Plateau-type problem with a sort of free boundary.
Precisely, after suitable projections, we will arrive to a Plateau-type problem on
the closed
rectangle
\overline{R}_{l}
, where

R_{l}:=(0,l)\times(-1,1)\times\{0\}

in Cartesian coordinates, equivalently

R_{l}=\{t\in(0,l),\;\rho\in[0,1),\;\theta=0\}\cup\{t\in(0,l),\;\rho\in[0,1),\;\theta=\pi\}
in cylindrical coordinates.
The rectangle
R_{l}
will be
often identified with

(0,l)\times(-1,1)
, thus neglecting the third coordinate.
We will impose a Dirichlet boundary condition
\varphi
on a part

\partial_{D}R_{l}:=(\{0\}\times[-1,1])\cup([0,l]\times\{-1\})

(11.1)

of
\partial R_{l}
, while no conditions will be imposed on
\{l\}\times(-1,1)
; more involved conditions will be assigned
on
(0,l)\times\{1\}
,
see the mutual relations between
\psi
and
h
in (
11.23
)
(see also the problem on the right-hand side of
(
11.25
) and Section
12
).

Then the strategy to estimate from below the relaxed area of the graph of the
vortex map
u
will be the following:
We split

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(u_{k},\Omega)=\int_{\Omega\setminus D_{k}}|\mathcal{M}(\nabla u_{k})|~dx+\int_{D_{k}}|\mathcal{M}(\nabla u_{k})|~dx.

In order to estimate the
\liminf_{k\to+\infty}
of the first term
on the right-hand side
we will employ (
4.37
),
whereas, in order to pass to the limit as
k\rightarrow+\infty
in
the second term, we will use
(
9.12
), so that we first want to render the right-hand side of this latter
inequality independent of
k
. This will be done with the aid
of the non-parametric Plateau-type problem studied in this section
and in Section
12
.

Definition 11.1

(
The projection
p
)
.

We let
p:C_{l}\cap\{t\geq 0\}\to\overline{R}_{l}
be the othogonal projection.

Recall that

\mathcal{G}_{\pm\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}

are defined in (
9.8
),
that
\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}
is the generalized polar graph of
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}
on its domain of definition (see (
9.5
)), and that
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}
takes values in
[0,\pi]
.
We first prove the following preliminary result:

Lemma 11.2
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
be as in (
4.4
), (
4.5
),
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then there is a negligible set
C_{k,\varepsilon}\subset(0,l)
such that
for all
t\in(0,l)\setminus C_{k,\varepsilon}

p\Big({\rm spt}(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})\Big)\cap(\{t\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})

(11.2)

is a subinterval of the segment
\overline{R}_{l}\cap(\{t\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})=\{t\}\times[-1,1]\times\{0\}

with one endpoint
(t,1,0)
.
Moreover
p({\rm spt}(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}))=p({\rm spt}(\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}))
.

Proof.

The latter assertion follows by symmetry. To prove the former,
we argue by slicing.
For a.e.
t\in(0,l)
the set
{\rm spt}(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})\cap(\{t\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
coincides with support of the current

(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})_{t}
, see
[
23
, Def. 7.6.2]
.
First notice that for all
t\in(0,\varepsilon)
the
conclusion
follows by construction
52
52

52

In this set
we have
\vartheta_{k,\varepsilon}=\pi
and the current
(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})_{t}
is the integration over the
half-circle
\{t\}\times((\partial B_{1})\cap\{\theta\in(0,\pi)\})
,
whose projection through
p
is
the whole interval with endpoints
(t,1,0)
and

(t,1,0)
(in cylindrical coordinates).
.

It remains to consider the case
t\in(\varepsilon,l)
.
Recall that
the set
S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}
in (
9.4
) has the form

\displaystyle\{t\in(\varepsilon,l),\;\rho\in[|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k}),1],\;\theta=0\}.

Therefore, for a.e.
t\in(\varepsilon,l)
the slice
(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})_{t}

is the integration over
{\rm spt}(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})

restricted to the plane
\{t\}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, which in turn is the
integration over the generalized polar graph (see (
2.8
))
of
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}
restricted to the closed set

\{t\}\times[|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k}),1]\times[0,\pi].

Namely

(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})_{t}=\text{{\rm\textlbrackdbl}}{{\rm spt}(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})\cap\big(\{t\}\times[|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k}),1]\times[0,\pi]\big)}\text{{\rm\textrbrackdbl}},

so that the support
\sigma_{t}
of
(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})_{t}
can also be
obtained as

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}=\bigcap_{h=1}^{+\infty}\sigma_{t}^{h},

(11.3)

where

\sigma_{t}^{h}:={\rm spt}(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})\cap\Big(\{t\}\times[\big(|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k})\big)-\frac{1}{h},1]\times[0,\pi]\Big).

For
h\in\mathbb{N}
large enough, let

U_{h}:=\{t\}\times\big((|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k}))-\frac{1}{h},1\big)\times(-\frac{1}{h},\pi+\frac{1}{h}),

which is a relatively open set in
\{t\}\times B_{1}
,
and let
(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})_{t}
be the slice of
\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}
on
\{t\}\times B_{1}
.
We have

\displaystyle{\rm spt}((\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})_{t}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits U_{h})\subset\sigma_{t}^{h}\subset\{t\}\times[\big(|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k})\big)-\frac{1}{h},1]\times[0,\pi].

(11.4)

On the other hand
since
\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits(\{t\}\times B_{1})
is the boundary of the subgraph of
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}
in
\{t\}\times B_{1}
, it is a closed
1
-integral current in
U_{h}
and
in
(\{t\}\times(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{(|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k}))-\frac{1}{h}})
,
so that the boundary
\partial((\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})_{t}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits U_{h})
in
\mathcal{D}_{1}(\{t\}\times B_{1})
is
supported on
(\partial U_{h})\cap((\partial B_{1})\cup\partial B_{(|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k}))-\frac{1}{h}})
.
From (
11.4
), the fact that
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}=0

at
(t,1)
and that
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}
is constant on the segment
\big((|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k}))-\frac{1}{h},|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k})\big)

with value either
0
or
\pi
, we deduce that

\displaystyle{\rm spt}(\partial((\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})_{t}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits U_{h}))

\displaystyle\subset\big(\{t\}\times\{(|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k}))-\frac{1}{h}\}\times\{0,\pi\}\big)\bigcup\big(\{t\}\times\{1\}\times\{0\}\big).

(11.5)

Moreover, if we set

P_{1}:=(t,1,0)\text{ and }P_{2}^{h}:=\big(t,(|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k}))-\frac{1}{h},\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}((|u_{k}|^{-}(t)\wedge(1-\lambda_{k}))-\frac{1}{h})\big),

from (
11
) it follows that

\partial((\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})_{t}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits U_{h})=\delta_{P_{2}^{h}}-\delta_{P_{1}}.

By decomposition of the integral
1
-current
(\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)})_{t}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=7.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=6.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits U_{h}
(see
[
15
, Section 4.2.25]
),
there are at most countable Lipschitz curves
\{\alpha_{i}^{h}\}
such that
\alpha^{h}_{0}
connects
P_{2}^{h}
to
P_{1}
, and
\alpha^{h}_{i}
is closed for
i>0
.
We claim that there cannot be closed curves
\alpha^{h}_{i}
, namely
\{\alpha^{h}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}=\{\alpha^{h}_{0}\}
.
Indeed, since
\alpha_{0}^{h}
connects
P_{1}
and
P_{2}^{h}
, we see that
(\{t\}\times\partial B_{\rho})\cap\alpha_{0}^{h}

consists of at least one point for

Remark 11.6

(i)

Lemma 11.7
.

We have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|+|\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|\geq

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}\left((\partial^{*}SG_{\psi_{k,\varepsilon}})\cap(R_{l}\times[0,+\infty)\right)+\mathcal{H}^{2}\left((\partial^{*}UG_{-\psi_{k,\varepsilon}})\cap(R_{l}\times(-\infty,0])\right)

(11.14)

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{H}^{2}(R_{l}\setminus SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}).

Moreover,

\mathcal{H}^{2}\left((\partial^{*}SG_{\psi_{k,\varepsilon}})\cap(R_{l}\times[0,+\infty))\right)=\mathcal{H}^{2}\left((\partial^{*}UG_{-\psi_{k,\varepsilon}})\cap(R_{l}\times(-\infty,0])\right)
.

Proof.

The last assertion follows by symmetry. Let us prove the former:
By (
11.13
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial^{*}SG_{\psi_{k,\varepsilon}}\cap(R_{l}\times[0,+\infty)))=

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}\big((\partial^{*}\mathbb{S}_{\rm cl}(O_{k,\varepsilon}))^{+}\big)+\mathcal{H}^{2}(\{(w_{1},w_{2})\in SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}:\psi_{k,\varepsilon}=0\})

\displaystyle+\mathcal{H}^{2}(R_{l}\setminus SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial^{*}UG_{-\psi_{k,\varepsilon}}\cap(R_{l}\times(-\infty,0]))=

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}\big((\partial^{*}\mathbb{S}_{\rm cl}(O_{k,\varepsilon}))^{-}\big)+\mathcal{H}^{2}(\{(w_{1},w_{2})\in SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}:\psi_{k,\varepsilon}=0\})

\displaystyle+\mathcal{H}^{2}(R_{l}\setminus SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}).

Taking the sum of these two expressions
and using (
11.9
), (
11.11
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial^{*}SG_{\psi_{k,\varepsilon}}\cap(R_{l}\times[0,+\infty)))+\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial^{*}UG_{-\psi_{k,\varepsilon}}\cap(R_{l}\times(-\infty,0]))

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}+\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|+2\mathcal{H}^{2}(\{(w_{1},w_{2})\in SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}:\psi_{k,\varepsilon}=0\})+2\mathcal{H}^{2}(R_{l}\setminus SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}).

Recalling (
9.4
),
we now claim that, up to
\mathcal{H}^{2}
-negligible sets,

\displaystyle\{(w_{1},w_{2})\in SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}:\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(w_{1},w_{2})=0\}\subset\big\{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}=0\big\}\cap S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon},

(11.15)

see Fig.
10
.
From the claim it follows that

\mathcal{H}^{2}(\{(w_{1},w_{2})\in SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}:\psi_{k,\varepsilon}=0\})\leq\mathcal{H}^{2}\big(\big\{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}\in\{0,\pi\}\big\}\cap S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}\big),

and hence by (
9.10
) we conclude

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial^{*}SG_{\psi_{k,\varepsilon}}\cap(R_{l}\times[0,+\infty)))+\mathcal{H}^{2}(\partial^{*}UG_{-\psi_{k,\varepsilon}}\cap(R_{l}\times(-\infty,0]))

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{G}_{\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|+|\mathcal{G}_{-\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}}^{(4)}|+2\mathcal{H}^{2}(R_{l}\setminus SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}),

that is (
11.14
).
It remains to show (
11.15
).
As usual, we argue by slicing; hence for almost all

w_{1}\in(0,l)
we will show that
(
11.15
) holds (up to
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible sets). Notice that both the left and right-hand sides of (
11.15
) are empty for
w_{1}<\varepsilon
, so we assume
w_{1}>\varepsilon
.
Therefore, fix
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})\in SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}
(with
\widetilde{w}_{1}>\varepsilon
) such
that
\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})=0
and assume, by contradiction, that
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})>0
.
In a first step we will suppose
\widetilde{w}_{2}<0
.
We might further assume that
\widetilde{w}_{2}
is a Lebesgue point for
the function
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{1},\cdot)
.
Hence
in any left-neighbourhood of this point
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}
is strictly positive on a set of positive meausure,
i.e.
,
we can find positive numbers
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}
such that
for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{1})
,
there exists a set
B\subset(\widetilde{w}_{2}-\delta,\widetilde{w}_{2})
,
of positive measure such that

\displaystyle\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{1},w)>\delta_{2}>0\qquad\forall w\in B.

(11.16)

If
\pi_{0}^{\rm pol}
is the projection in Definition (
7.5
), since
\widetilde{w}_{2}<0
for
\delta_{3}>0
small enough the segment
I:=\{(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2},w_{3}):w_{3}\in(0,\delta_{3})\}
satisfies

\displaystyle I_{0}:=\pi_{0}^{\rm pol}(I)\subset\{(\widetilde{w}_{1},w_{2},0):w_{2}\in(\widetilde{w}_{2}-\delta_{2},\widetilde{w}_{2})\}.

We have that
\pi_{0}^{\rm pol}:I\rightarrow I_{0}
is a homeomorphism.
Now, if
\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})=0
the segment
I

cannot intersect the subgraph of
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}
(on a set of positive
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-measure), and thus

\displaystyle\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{1},w_{2})\leq\theta\big(({\pi_{0}^{\rm pol}}_{|I_{0}})^{-1}(\widetilde{w}_{1},w_{2},0)\big)\qquad\text{ for $\mathcal{H}^{1}$-a.e. }(\widetilde{w}_{1},w_{2},0)\in I_{0},

(11.17)

where
\theta
represents the usual angular coordinate. Since

\theta\big(({\pi_{0}^{\rm pol}}_{|I_{0}})^{-1}(\widetilde{w}_{1},w_{2},0)\big)
is infinitesimal as
w_{2}\rightarrow\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-}
,
condition (
11.17
) contradicts (
11.16
).

Let us now treat the case
\widetilde{w}_{2}>0
.
This is much simpler to deal with,
up to noticing that
\widehat{\vartheta}_{k,\varepsilon}
is defined on
S^{(4)}_{k,\varepsilon}\subset\{(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3}):w_{2}\in[-1,0]\}
. The fact that
\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})=0
means
that the line
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})\times\mathbb{R}

does not intersect
O_{k,\varepsilon}
on a set of positive
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-measure
but this contradicts the fact that
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})\in SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}
.
Indeed since
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})\in SG_{h_{k,\varepsilon}}
hence there exists
w_{2}>\widetilde{w}_{2}
such that
\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{1},w_{2})>0
. Let
A:=O_{k,\varepsilon}\cap(\widetilde{w}_{1},w_{2})\times\mathbb{R})
then a suitable
rotation of
A

around the axis of the cylinder shall meet
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})\times\mathbb{R}
on a set
\widetilde{A}
of positive
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-measure (note that
\widetilde{A}\subset O_{k,\varepsilon}
), which contradicts
\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})=0.

∎

\displaystyle{\rm the~trace~of~}\psi_{k,\varepsilon}{\rm~on~}\overline{R}_{l}\cap\{w_{1}=0\}{\rm~is~}\sqrt{1-w_{2}^{2}},\quad{\rm for}\;w_{2}\in[-1,1].

\displaystyle{\psi_{k,\varepsilon}}(w_{1},-1)=0\;\;\;\text{ and }\;\;\;{\psi_{k,\varepsilon}}(w_{1},1)=0,\qquad w_{1}\in(0,l).

(11.19)

Remark 11.9
.

We can write
[
19
]

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}\Big((\partial^{*}SG_{\psi_{k,\varepsilon}})\cap(R_{l}\times[0,\infty))\Big)=\mathbb{A}(\psi_{k,\varepsilon},R_{l}),

(11.20)

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}(\psi_{k,\varepsilon},R_{l})=\int_{R_{l}}\sqrt{1+|\nabla\psi_{k,\varepsilon}|^{2}}~dx+|D^{s}\psi_{k,\varepsilon}|(R_{l})

is the classical area of the graph of the
BV
-function
\psi_{k,\varepsilon}
in
R_{l}
.
Moreover, by (
