) for the area hold on
({\rm B}_{r_{k}}\setminus{\rm B}_{r_{k}/2})\cap C_{k}^{-}
.

Step 4.
Definition of
{u_{k}}
on
{{C}_{k}}\cap{\rm B}_{r_{k}/2}

and its area contribution.

We start with the construction of
u_{k}
on
C_{k}^{+}\cap{\rm B}_{r_{k}/2}
.
For
r\in[0,r_{k}/2)
and
\theta\in[0,\theta_{k}]
we set

\displaystyle u_{k}(r,\theta):=\Upsilon_{k}\Big(\frac{4r\theta}{r_{k}\theta_{k}}-1,\psi_{k}^{\star}\big(0,1-\frac{4r\theta}{r_{k}\theta_{k}}\big)\Big).

(13.31)

First we observe that

u_{k}\Big(\frac{r_{k}}{2},\theta\Big):=\Upsilon_{k}\Big(\frac{2\theta}{\theta_{k}}-1,\psi_{k}^{\star}\big(0,1-\frac{2\theta}{\theta_{k}}\big)\Big),\qquad\theta\in(0,\theta_{k}),

so that
u_{k}
is continuous on
C_{k}^{+}\cap\partial{\rm B}_{r_{k}/2}
(see (
13.28
) and (
13.16
)), and

\displaystyle u_{k}(r,\theta_{k})=\Upsilon_{k}\Big(\frac{4r}{r_{k}}-1,\psi_{k}^{\star}(0,1-\frac{4r}{r_{k}})\Big),

(13.32)

\displaystyle u_{k}(r,0)=(-1,\psi_{k}^{\star}(0,1))=(-1,0).

(13.33)

Direct computations lead to the following estimates:

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}u_{k}(r,\theta)|\leq\widehat{C}\frac{4\theta}{r_{k}\theta_{k}}\Big(1+|\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k}(0,1-\frac{4\theta r}{r_{k}\theta_{k}})|\Big),

(13.34)

\displaystyle|\partial_{\theta}u_{k}(r,\theta)|\leq\widehat{C}\frac{4r}{r_{k}\theta_{k}}\Big(1+|\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k}(0,1-\frac{4\theta r}{r_{k}\theta_{k}})|\Big),

(13.35)

where
\widehat{C}
is the constant bounding the gradient of
\Upsilon_{k}
as in
step 3.
Finally, since by (
13.31
)
u_{k}
takes values in
\mathbb{S}^{1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we have

Ju_{k}(r,\theta)=0
for all
r\in(0,r_{k}/2)
,
\theta\in[0,\theta_{k}]
.
Hence, the area of the graph of
u_{k}
on
C_{k}^{+}\cap{\rm B}_{r_{k}/2}
is

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r_{k}/2}\int_{0}^{\theta_{k}}r|\mathcal{M}(\nabla u_{k})(r,\theta)|~d\theta dr\leq\int_{0}^{r_{k}/2}\int_{0}^{\theta_{k}}(r+C)+\frac{C}{\theta_{k}}+\frac{Cr}{r_{k}}(1+\frac{1}{\theta_{k}})|\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k}(0,1-\frac{4\theta r}{r_{k}\theta_{k}})|d\theta dr,

where

C
is a positive constant independent of
k
. Exploiting
that
|\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k}(0,\cdot)|\leq|\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}(0,\cdot)|
, we can
estimate the right-hand side of the previous formula as follows:

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{r_{k}/2}\int_{0}^{\theta_{k}}\frac{r}{r_{k}}\Big(1+\frac{1}{\theta_{k}}\Big)|\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}(0,1-\frac{4\theta r}{r_{k}\theta_{k}})|d\theta dr+o(1)

(13.36)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{r_{k}/2}\int_{-1}^{1}\theta_{k}\Big(1+\frac{1}{\theta_{k}}\Big)|\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}(0,w_{2})|dw_{2}dr+o(1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{r_{k}/2}(\theta_{k}+1)dr+o(1)=o(1),

where
o(1)\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow+\infty
, and
C
is a positive constant independent of
k
which might change from line to line.

In
C_{k}^{-}\cap{\rm B}_{r_{k}/2}
we set, for
r\in[0,r_{k}/2)
,
\theta\in[2\pi-\theta_{k},2\pi)
,

\displaystyle u_{k}(r,\theta):=(u_{k1}(r,2\pi-\theta),-u_{k2}(r,2\pi-\theta)).

Similar estimates as in (
13.36
)
for the area hold on
C_{k}^{-}\cap{\rm B}_{r_{k}/2}
.

Step 5.
Definition of
{u_{k}}
on
T_{k}

and its area contribution.

We first construct
u_{k}
on
T_{k}\cap\{(r,\theta):r\in[0,r_{k}/2],\theta\in[\theta_{k},\overline{\theta}_{k}]\}
.
We define
\beta_{k}:[0,r_{k}/2]\times[\theta_{k},\overline{\theta}_{k}]\rightarrow[0,\pi]
as

\displaystyle\beta_{k}(r,\theta):=\frac{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}}\alpha_{k}(r)+(1-\frac{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}})\big(\frac{2r}{r_{k}}(\overline{\theta}_{k}-\pi)+\pi\big),

where

\alpha_{k}(r):=\arccos\big(\Upsilon_{k1}(\frac{4r}{r_{k}}-1,\psi^{\star}_{k}(0,1-\frac{4r}{r_{k}}))\big),\qquad r\in[0,r_{k}/2].

Notice that
\alpha_{k}
is decreasing and takes values in

[0,\pi]
.
Therefore we set

\displaystyle u_{k}(r,\theta):=\big(\cos(\beta_{k}(r,\theta)),\sin(\beta_{k}(r,\theta))\big),\qquad(r,\theta)\in[0,r_{k}/2]\times[\theta_{k},\overline{\theta}_{k}].

One checks that
\beta_{k}(r,\theta_{k})=\alpha_{k}(r)
,

\beta_{k}(r,\overline{\theta}_{k})=\frac{2r}{r_{k}}(\overline{\theta}_{k}-\pi)+\pi

(see also (
13.9
)), and

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}(r_{k}/2)=0,

\displaystyle u_{k}(r_{k}/2,\theta)=\big(\cos((1-\frac{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}})\overline{\theta}_{k}),\sin((1-\frac{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}})\overline{\theta}_{k})\big),

\displaystyle u_{k}(r,\theta_{k})=\big(\cos(\alpha_{k}(r)),\sin(\alpha_{k}(r))\big)=\Upsilon_{k}(\frac{4r}{r_{k}}-1,\psi^{\star}_{k}(0,1-\frac{4r}{r_{k}})),

\displaystyle u_{k}(r,\overline{\theta}_{k})=\big(\cos(\frac{2r}{r_{k}}(\overline{\theta}_{k}-\pi)+\pi),\sin(\frac{2r}{r_{k}}(\overline{\theta}_{k}-\pi)+\pi)\big),

so that
u_{k}
is continuous on
\{\theta\in\{\theta_{k},\overline{\theta}_{k}\},\;r\in[0,r_{k}/2]\}\cap\Omega
,
see (
13.10
) and (
13.32
).

Notice also that
u_{k}
is continuous
at
(0,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
u_{k}(0,0)=(-1,0)
.
Finally, since
u_{k}
takes values in
\mathbb{S}^{1}
, the
determinant of its Jacobian vanishes,
so that in order to estimate the area contribution
of the graph of
u_{k}
in
T_{k}\cap\{(r,\theta):r\in[0,r_{k}/2],\;\theta\in[\theta_{k},\overline{\theta}_{k}]\}
it is sufficient to estimate the derivatives of
u_{k}
. We have

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}u_{k}(r,\theta)|=|\partial_{r}\beta_{k}(r,\theta)|\leq|\partial_{r}\alpha_{k}(r)|+\frac{2\pi}{r_{k}},

\displaystyle|\partial_{\theta}u_{k}(r,\theta)|=|\partial_{\theta}\beta_{k}(r,\theta)|\leq\frac{|\alpha_{k}(r)|}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}}+\frac{\pi}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}}\leq\frac{2\pi}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r_{k}/2}\int_{\theta_{k}}^{\overline{\theta}_{k}}r|\mathcal{M}(\nabla u_{k})(r,\theta)|d\theta dr\leq\int_{0}^{r_{k}/2}\int_{\theta_{k}}^{\overline{\theta}_{k}}\Big[\frac{r_{k}}{2}(1+|\partial_{r}\beta_{k}(r,\theta)|)+|\partial_{\theta}\beta_{k}(r,\theta)|\Big]~d\theta dr

\displaystyle\leq o(1)+\int_{0}^{r_{k}/2}\int_{\theta_{k}}^{\overline{\theta}_{k}}\left(\frac{r_{k}}{2}|\partial_{r}\alpha_{k}(r)|+\pi+\frac{2\pi}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}}\right)d\theta dr=o(1),

(13.37)

with
o(1)\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow+\infty
.
Notice that the integral of
|\partial_{r}\alpha_{k}(r)|
with respect to
r
can be computed via the fundamental integration theorem, since
\alpha_{k}

is monotone.

In
T_{k}\cap\{(r,\theta):r\in[0,r_{k}/2],\theta\in[2\pi-\overline{\theta}_{k},2\pi-\theta_{k}]\}
we set

\displaystyle u_{k}(r,\theta):=(u_{k1}(r,2\pi-\theta),-u_{k2}(r,2\pi-\theta)).

We now define
u_{k}
on
T_{k}\cap\{(r,\theta):r\in(r_{k}/2,l),\theta\in[\theta_{k},\overline{\theta}_{k}]\}
. We set

u_{k}(r,\theta):=\big(\cos((1-\frac{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}})\overline{\theta}_{k}),\sin((1-\frac{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}})\overline{\theta}_{k})\big).

Then
{u_{k}}\in\textrm{Lip}(T_{k},\mathbb{S}^{1})
, and

\displaystyle{u_{k}}(r,\theta_{k})=(1,0),\qquad{u_{k}}(r,\overline{\theta}_{k})=(\cos\overline{\theta}_{k},\sin\overline{\theta}_{k})\quad\text{ for }r\in({r_{k}}/2,l),

\displaystyle\partial_{r}u_{k}(r,\theta)=0,

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta}u_{k}(r,\theta)=\frac{\overline{\theta}_{k}}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}}\Big(-\sin((1-\frac{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}})\overline{\theta}_{k}),\cos((1-\frac{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}})\overline{\theta}_{k})\Big).

Hence

\displaystyle\int_{r_{k}/2}^{l}\int_{\theta_{k}}^{\overline{\theta}_{k}}r|\mathcal{M}(\nabla u_{k})(r,\theta)|d\theta dr\leq\int_{r_{k}/2}^{l}\int_{\theta_{k}}^{\overline{\theta}_{k}}\Big(r+\frac{\overline{\theta}_{k}}{\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k}}\Big)~d\theta dr=o(1)

(13.38)

as
k\rightarrow+\infty
.

Finally in

T_{k}\cap\{(r,\theta):r\in(r_{k}/2,l),\theta\in[2\pi-\overline{\theta}_{k},2\pi-\theta_{k}]\}
we set

\displaystyle u_{k}(r,\theta):=(u_{k1}(r,2\pi-\theta),-u_{k2}(r,2\pi-\theta)).

Similar estimates as in (
13
),
(
13.38
) for the area hold on

T_{k}\cap\{(r,\theta):r\in(0,r_{k}/2),\theta\in[2\pi-\overline{\theta}_{k},2\pi-\theta_{k}]\}
,

T_{k}\cap\{(r,\theta):r\in(r_{k}/2,l),\theta\in[2\pi-\overline{\theta}_{k},2\pi-\theta_{k}]\}
,
respectively.

Step 6.
We claim that

\int_{{\Omega}\setminus(C_{k}\cup T_{k})}|\mathcal{M}(\nabla{u_{k}})|dx\longrightarrow\int_{\Omega}|\mathcal{M}(\nabla u)|~dx\qquad\text{ as }k\to+\infty,

(13.39)

where we recall that
C_{k}\cup T_{k}=\{(r,\theta)\in{\Omega}:r\in[0,l),\theta\in[0,\overline{\theta}_{k}]\cup[2\pi-\overline{\theta}_{k},2\pi)\}.

Indeed, on
{\Omega}\setminus(C_{k}\cup T_{k})

the maps
{u_{k}}
and
u
take values in the circle
\mathbb{S}^{1}
, hence

\det(\nabla{u_{k}})=0,\qquad\det(\nabla u)=0,\qquad\text{ in }{\Omega}\setminus(C_{k}\cup T_{k}).

Thus

\int_{{\Omega}\setminus(C_{k}\cup T_{k})}|\mathcal{M}(\nabla{u_{k}})-\mathcal{M}(\nabla u)|~dx\leq\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{{\Omega}\setminus(C_{k}\cup T_{k})}|\nabla({u_{k}}_{i}-u_{i})|~dx.

From (
13.9
), we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}({u_{k}}-u)|=0\qquad\textrm{ in }\quad{\Omega}\setminus({{\rm B}_{r_{k}}}\cup{{C}_{k}}\cup T_{k}),

(13.40)

\displaystyle|\partial_{r}({u_{k}}-u)|\leq\frac{\pi}{{r_{k}}}\ \quad\textrm{ in }\quad{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}}\setminus({{C}_{k}}\cup T_{k}),

\displaystyle|\partial_{\theta}({u_{k}}-u)|=0\qquad\text{ in }\quad{\Omega}\setminus({{\rm B}_{r_{k}}}\cup{{C}_{k}}\cup T_{k}),

\displaystyle|\partial_{\theta}({u_{k}}-u)|\leq 2\qquad\textrm{ in }\quad{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}}\setminus({{C}_{k}}\cup T_{k}).

Our previous remarks and the fact that
{r_{k}},\theta_{k},(\overline{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{k})\to 0^{+}
as
k\to+\infty
, imply (
13.39
).

Step 7.

We know from (
13
), (
13.36
), (
13
), and (
13.38
), that the integral of
|\mathcal{M}(\nabla u_{k})|
is infinitesimal as
k\rightarrow+\infty
, on the region
({\rm B}_{r_{k}}\cap C_{k})\cup T_{k}
. Therefore it
remains to compute the area of the graphs of
u_{k}
in the region
C_{k}\setminus{\rm B}_{r_{k}}
.
We claim that this contribution is

\lim_{k\to+\infty}\int_{{{C}_{k}}\setminus{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}}}|\mathcal{M}(\nabla{u_{k}})|~dx\leq 2{\mathcal{F}}_{l}({h^{\star}},\psi^{\star})=\mathbb{A}(\psi^{\star},SG_{{h^{\star}}}).

(13.41)

To prove this, we start to compute the area of the graph of
{u_{k}}
restricted to
C_{k}^{+}\setminus{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}}
.
From (
13.25
), (
13.17
), (
13.19
) and (
13.18
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{r}{u_{k}}_{1}=\Big(\frac{\theta}{\theta_{k}}-1\Big)\tau_{k}^{\prime}{h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}=\frac{l}{l-{r_{k}}}\Big(\frac{\theta}{\theta_{k}}-1\Big){h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime},

(13.42)

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta}{u_{k}}_{1}=\frac{1+{h^{\star}_{k}}}{\theta_{k}},

\displaystyle\partial_{r}{u_{k}}_{2}=\tau_{k}^{\prime}\Big[\Big(1-\frac{\theta}{\theta_{k}}\Big){h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k}+\partial_{w_{1}}\psi^{\star}_{k}\Big]=\frac{l}{l-{r_{k}}}\Big[\Big(1-\frac{\theta}{\theta_{k}}\Big){h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k}+\partial_{w_{1}}\psi^{\star}_{k}\Big],

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta}{u_{k}}_{2}=-\Big[\frac{1+{h^{\star}_{k}}}{\theta_{k}}\Big]\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k},

\displaystyle\partial_{r}{u_{k}}_{1}\partial_{\theta}{u_{k}}_{2}-\partial_{\theta}{u_{k}}_{1}\partial_{r}{u_{k}}_{2}=-\left(\frac{1+{h^{\star}_{k}}}{\theta_{k}}\right)\frac{l}{l-{r_{k}}}\partial_{w_{1}}\psi^{\star}_{k},

where
{h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}
denotes the
derivative of
{h^{\star}_{k}}
with respect
to
{w_{1}}
,

{h^{\star}_{k}},{h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}

are evaluated at

\tau_{k}(r)
, and the two partial derivatives

\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k}
,

\partial_{w_{1}}\psi^{\star}_{k}

of
\psi^{\star}_{k}
with respect to
{w_{2}},{w_{1}}

are evaluated at
(\tau_{k}(r),-s_{k}(r,\theta))
. Note carefully
that, in the computation of the Jacobian, the terms containing
\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k}
cancel each other.

Notice that, since
{h^{\star}_{k}}
is convex, its derivative is nonincreasing, and therefore

\int_{{r_{k}}}^{l}|{h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}|~dr<+\infty
.
As a consequence of (
13.42
), from (
2.3
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}({u_{k}},C_{k}^{+}\setminus{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{r_{k}}}^{l}\int_{0}^{\theta_{k}}r\Bigg\{1+\left(\frac{l}{l-{r_{k}}}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\theta}{\theta_{k}}-1\right)^{2}({h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime})^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{l}{l-{r_{k}}}\right)^{2}\left[\big(\frac{\theta}{\theta_{k}}-1\big)^{2}({h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime})^{2}(\partial_{{w_{2}}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}+2\big(1-\frac{\theta}{\theta_{k}}\big){h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}\partial_{{w_{2}}}\psi^{\star}_{k}\partial_{{w_{1}}}\psi^{\star}_{k}+(\partial_{{w_{1}}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\frac{1+{h^{\star}_{k}}}{\theta_{k}}\right)^{2}\left(1+(\partial_{{w_{2}}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}+\left(\frac{l}{l-{r_{k}}}\right)^{2}(\partial_{{w_{1}}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}\right)\Bigg\}^{\frac{1}{2}}~drd\theta,

where

\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k}
,
\partial_{{w_{1}}}\psi^{\star}_{k}

are evaluated at
(\tau_{k}(r),-s_{k}(r,\theta))
,
and
{h^{\star}_{k}}
,
{h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}
are evaluated at
\tau_{k}(r)
.
Now we use the change of variable (
13.22
):
from (
13.24
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}({u_{k}},C_{k}^{+}\setminus{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{l}\int^{{h^{\star}_{k}}({w_{1}})}_{-1}\left(\frac{l-{r_{k}}}{l}\right)\left(\frac{\theta_{k}}{1+{h^{\star}_{k}}}\right)R_{k}({w_{1}})\Bigg\{1+\big(\frac{l}{l-{r_{k}}}\big)^{2}\left(\frac{\Theta_{k}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})}{\theta_{k}}-1\right)^{2}({h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime})^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{l}{l-{r_{k}}}\right)^{2}\Big[\big(1-\frac{\Theta_{k}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})}{\theta_{k}}\big)^{2}({h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime})^{2}(\partial_{{w_{2}}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}+2\big(1-\frac{\Theta_{k}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})}{\theta_{k}}\big){h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}\partial_{{w_{2}}}\psi^{\star}_{k}\partial_{{w_{1}}}\psi^{\star}_{k}+(\partial_{{w_{1}}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(R_{k}({w_{1}}))^{2}}\big(\frac{1+{h^{\star}_{k}}}{\theta_{k}}\big)^{2}\Big(1+(\partial_{{w_{2}}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}+\big(\frac{l}{l-{r_{k}}}\big)^{2}(\partial_{{w_{1}}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}\Big)\Bigg\}^{\frac{1}{2}}~d{w_{2}}d{w_{1}},

where
R_{k}({w_{1}})
,

\Theta_{k}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})

are defined in (
13.20
), (
13.21
),
{h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}
is evaluated at
w_{1}
, and
\partial_{w_{1}}\psi^{\star}_{k}
and
\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k}
are evaluated at
(w_{1},w_{2})
.
Therefore

\mathcal{A}({u_{k}},C_{k}^{+}\setminus{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}})=\int_{0}^{l}\int^{{h^{\star}_{k}}({w_{1}})}_{-1}\Big\{\textrm{I}_{k}+\textrm{II}_{k}+\textrm{III}_{k}+\textrm{IV}_{k}+\textrm{V}_{k}+\textrm{VI}_{k}\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}~d{w_{2}}d{w_{1}},

(13.43)

where

\begin{cases}\textrm{I}_{k}=\left(\frac{l-{r_{k}}}{l}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\theta_{k}}{1+{h^{\star}_{k}}}\right)^{2}(R_{k}({w_{1}}))^{2},\\
\\
\textrm{II}_{k}=\left(\frac{\theta_{k}}{1+{h^{\star}_{k}}}\right)^{2}\left(1-\frac{\Theta_{k}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})}{\theta_{k}}\right)^{2}(R_{k}({w_{1}}))^{2}({h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime})^{2},\\
\\
\textrm{III}_{k}=\left(\frac{\theta_{k}}{1+{h^{\star}_{k}}}\right)^{2}(R_{k}({w_{1}}))^{2}\Big[\big(1-\frac{\Theta_{k}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})}{\theta_{k}}\big)^{2}({h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime})^{2}(\partial_{{w_{2}}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}\\
\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\big(1-\frac{\Theta_{k}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})}{\theta_{k}}\big){h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}\partial_{{w_{2}}}\psi^{\star}_{k}\partial_{{w_{1}}}\psi^{\star}_{k}+(\partial_{{w_{1}}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}\Big],\\
\\
\textrm{IV}_{k}=\big(\frac{l-{r_{k}}}{l}\big)^{2},\\
\\
\textrm{V}_{k}=\big(\frac{l-{r_{k}}}{l}\big)^{2}(\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2},\\
\\
\textrm{VI}_{k}=(\partial_{{w_{1}}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}.\end{cases}

Since

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{l-{r_{k}}}{l}=1

and
\lim_{k\to+\infty}\theta_{k}=0
, we deduce
from (
13.20
), (
13.21
),

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to+\infty}R_{k}({w_{1}})={w_{1}},\qquad\lim_{k\to+\infty}\frac{\Theta_{k}({w_{1}},{w_{2}})}{\theta_{k}}=\frac{{h^{\star}}({w_{1}})-{w_{2}}}{1+{h^{\star}}({w_{1}})}.

Therefore we see that

\int_{0}^{l}\int^{{h^{\star}_{k}}({w_{1}})}_{-1}(\textrm{I}_{k})^{\frac{1}{2}}+(\textrm{II}_{k})^{\frac{1}{2}}dw_{2}dw_{1}=o(1),

as
k\rightarrow+\infty
.
Moreover

\int_{0}^{l}\int^{{h^{\star}_{k}}({w_{1}})}_{-1}(\textrm{III}_{k})^{\frac{1}{2}}dw_{2}dw_{1}=o(1)

(13.44)

as
k\rightarrow+\infty
.
Indeed
we may estimate

\int_{0}^{l}\int^{{h^{\star}_{k}}({w_{1}})}_{-1}(\textrm{III}_{k})^{\frac{1}{2}}dw_{2}dw_{1}\leq C\theta_{k}\int_{0}^{l}\int^{{h^{\star}_{k}}({w_{1}})}_{-1}|{h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}(w_{1})||\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k}(w_{1},w_{2})|+|\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k}(w_{1},w_{2})|dw_{2}dw_{1},

and using that
|{h^{\star}_{k}}^{\prime}(w_{1})|\leq 2k
(see (
13.11
)),
if we assume (
13.5
),
i.e.
,

\theta_{k}k\rightarrow 0
, then
(
13.44
) follows,
since the
BV
-norm of
\psi_{k}^{\star}
is bounded uniformly with respect to
k
.

Hence, from (
13.43
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}({u_{k}},C_{k}^{+}\setminus{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{l}\int^{{h^{\star}_{k}}({w_{1}})}_{-1}\Big\{\textrm{IV}_{k}+\textrm{V}_{k}+\textrm{VI}_{k}\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}~d{w_{2}}d{w_{1}}+o(1)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{l}\int^{{h^{\star}_{k}}({w_{1}})}_{-1}\sqrt{1+(\partial_{{w_{1}}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}+(\partial_{w_{2}}\psi^{\star}_{k})^{2}}~d{w_{2}}dw_{1}+o(1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{A}(\psi^{\star}_{k},SG_{h^{\star}}\cap R_{l})+o(1)=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{A}(\psi^{\star}_{k},SG_{h^{\star}})+o(1)

(13.45)

as
k\rightarrow+\infty
.
Then taking the limit as
k\to+\infty
in (
13
), and using Lemma
13.1
(iii), we get

\lim_{k\to+\infty}\mathcal{A}({u_{k}},C_{k}^{+}\setminus{{\rm B}_{r_{k}}})\leq\mathbb{A}(\psi^{\star},SG_{{h^{\star}}})={\mathcal{F}}_{2l}({h^{\star}},\psi^{\star}),

(13.46)

where the last equality follows from (
12.16
).

Step 8.
Conclusion.
Notice that
{u_{k}}\in{\rm Lip}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
and
u_{k}\to u
in
L^{1}({\Omega},\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
Inequality
(
13.4
) follows from
(
13.39
) (which gives the term
\int_{\Omega}|\mathcal{M}(\nabla u)|dx
), from (
13.41
) (which gives the second term in (
13.4
)), and from estimates (
13
), (
13.36
), (
13
), and (
13.38
), showing that all the other contributions are negligible.
∎

Corollary 13.4

(
Minimizers for
l
large enough
)
.

For
l
large enough, a
solution to
the minimum
problem on the right-hand side of (
12.9
)
is given by
h\equiv-1
and
\psi\equiv 0
.

Proof.

Recall that for
l
large enough, we have

\overline{\mathcal{A}}(u,\Omega)=\int_{{\Omega}}|\mathcal{M}(\nabla u)|dx+\pi,

see
[
1
]
. The assertion then follows
from Theorem
11.16
and
(
12.10
).
∎
